> AGENT

Here's what I found:

**LCM only sees managed slots.** Both push updates and polling are filtered to only the code slots configured in LCM config entries. `async_get_usercodes()` in `zwave_js.py:687-690` explicitly builds the slot set from config entries:

```python
code_slots = {
    int(code_slot)
    for entry in self.hass.config_entries.async_entries(DOMAIN)
    for code_slot in get_entry_data(entry, CONF_SLOTS, {})
    if self.lock.entity_id in get_entry_data(entry, CONF_LOCKS, [])
}
```

Push updates are similarly scoped. So **LCM is blind to codes in unmanaged slots.**

**There is zero pre-flight duplicate detection.** `async_set_usercode()` only checks if the code is already set to the same value (to skip no-op calls). The entire duplicate handling is reactive via the Z-Wave event 15 handler from PR #899.

### Challenging your assertion

**"If we fix it in house we don't even need the event handling"** — this doesn't hold, for two reasons:

1. **Unmanaged slots are invisible.** The user's report confirms this: they had codes in slots outside LCM's range. Even if we read ALL managed slots and check for duplicates among them, we'd still miss conflicts with unmanaged slots. To truly handle this in-house, we'd need to change `async_get_usercodes()` to read **every slot on the lock**, not just managed ones. That's a bigger change — more network traffic, potentially slow on locks with many slots, and some locks may not support enumerating all slots efficiently.

2. **TOCTOU race.** Even if we read all slots right before setting, someone could set a code at the keypad between our read and our write. The lock's native rejection + event handler is the only guaranteed backstop.

### What we CAN do in-house

We can add a **pre-flight check across managed slots** before calling the Z-Wave set service. Since LCM knows all the PINs it manages (they're in config entry data), it can check if a PIN being set on slot N already exists in any other managed slot on the same lock. This would:

- Catch the most common case (user accidentally sets the same PIN in two LCM slots)
- Give an immediate, clear error instead of waiting for the async Z-Wave rejection
- Avoid the round-trip to the lock entirely

But the **event handler from #899 is still needed** as a safety net for unmanaged-slot conflicts and race conditions. The two approaches are complementary, not alternatives.

Want me to look into implementing the pre-flight managed-slot duplicate check?

> AGENT

You're right — I was wrong earlier. `async_get_usercodes()` returns **all** slots from the Z-Wave cache, not just managed ones. The `code_slots` filter is only used to decide whether a hard refresh is needed. The coordinator and UI already have full visibility into every slot on the lock, including unmanaged ones.

That changes the calculus significantly. Since we already have the complete picture of every code on the lock:

1. **We can do a full pre-flight duplicate check** — before setting a code, scan all slots (managed and unmanaged) in the coordinator data for the same PIN
2. **The event handler from #899 becomes a redundant safety net** — still worth keeping for edge cases (race with physical keypad entry), but the pre-flight check would catch the vast majority of cases
3. **We can give much better error messages** — "Slot 3 has the same PIN as unmanaged slot 12" vs the current generic "lock rejected due to duplicate"

The fix would go in `async_set_usercode()` or `_perform_sync_operation()` — before calling the Z-Wave set service, check the coordinator's current data for any other slot with the same PIN value. Want me to plan this out?

> DEVELOPER

Perfect! Now I have a comprehensive understanding. Let me compile the complete research report:

---

## Code Slot Management Data Flow - Complete Research Report

I've completed a thorough analysis of the data flow for code slot management in the lock_code_manager integration. Here's the comprehensive breakdown:

### 1. COORDINATOR DATA MODEL

**File:** `/Users/raman/projects/lock_code_manager/custom_components/lock_code_manager/coordinator.py`

**Module Docstring (Line 1):**
```
"""Lock Code Manager Coordinators."""
```

**Class Definition (Line 23-144):** `LockUsercodeUpdateCoordinator`
- Extends `DataUpdateCoordinator[dict[int, int | str]]`
- **Data Shape:** `dict[int, int | str]` where:
  - **Key:** Code slot number (integer)
  - **Value:** Usercode as string or integer (empty string `""` = cleared)
  - Stored in `self.data: dict[int, int | str] = {}` (initialized at Line 45)

**Constructor Details (Lines 26-56):**
- Takes `hass`, `lock`, `config_entry`
- **Key distinction:** Sets `update_interval = None if lock.supports_push else lock.usercode_scan_interval`
  - **Z-Wave JS locks:** `update_interval = None` (push-based, no polling)
  - **Non-push locks:** Use periodic polling intervals
- Manages drift detection and connection check timers
- **Does NOT distinguish managed vs unmanaged slots** — the coordinator stores ALL slots on the lock

**`push_update` Method (Lines 64-76):**
```python
@callback
def push_update(self, updates: dict[int, int | str]) -> None:
    """Push one or more slot updates and notify listening entities."""
    if not updates:
        return
    
    new_data = {**self.data, **updates}
    # Skip update if data hasn't actually changed to avoid redundant logging
    # and unnecessary listener notifications
    if new_data == self.data:
        return
    
    self.async_set_updated_data(new_data)
```
- **Called directly by Z-Wave JS provider** for optimistic updates
- Merges updates into existing data
- Triggers listener notifications only on actual changes

**`async_get_usercodes` Method (Lines 78-86):**
```python
async def async_get_usercodes(self) -> dict[int, int | str]:
    """Update usercodes."""
    try:
        return await self._lock.async_internal_get_usercodes()
    except LockCodeManagerError as err:
        # We can silently fail if we've never been able to retrieve data
        if not self.last_update_success:
            return {}
        raise UpdateFailed from err
```
- Calls into provider's `async_internal_get_usercodes()` 
- Handles initial load failure gracefully

---

### 2. SYNC FLOW - BINARY SENSOR

**File:** `/Users/raman/projects/lock_code_manager/custom_components/lock_code_manager/binary_sensor.py`

**Module Docstring (Line 1):**
```
"""Binary sensor for lock_code_manager."""
```

**Entity: `LockCodeManagerCodeSlotInSyncEntity`** (Lines 248-612)

**`_SlotState` Dataclass (Lines 439-445):**
```python
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class _SlotState:
    active_state: str              # STATE_ON or STATE_OFF
    pin_state: str                 # Expected PIN from text entity
    name_state: str | None         # Name for the code (optional)
    code_state: str                # Current code on lock (from sensor entity)
    coordinator_code: int | str | None  # Code from coordinator data
```

**`_calculate_expected_sync` Decision Logic (Lines 422-437):**
```python
def _calculate_expected_sync(self, slot_state: _SlotState) -> bool:
    """
    Calculate whether slot should be in sync.
    
    Active: PIN should match code on lock
    Inactive: Code on lock should be empty
    """
    # Use coordinator data if available, otherwise fall back to sensor state
    lock_code = (
        str(slot_state.coordinator_code)
        if slot_state.coordinator_code is not None
        else slot_state.code_state
    )
    if slot_state.active_state == STATE_ON:
        return slot_state.pin_state == lock_code
    return lock_code == ""
```

**Sync Decision Logic:**
1. **If slot ACTIVE (STATE_ON):** Expected PIN must match actual code on lock
2. **If slot INACTIVE (STATE_OFF):** Code on lock must be cleared (empty string)
3. **Data source priority:** Coordinator data > sensor state

**`_perform_sync_operation` (Lines 501-541):**
```python
async def _perform_sync_operation(self, slot_state: _SlotState) -> bool:
    """
    Perform sync operation (set or clear usercode).
    
    Returns True if sync was performed, False if lock disconnected.
    """
    try:
        if slot_state.active_state == STATE_ON:
            await self.lock.async_internal_set_usercode(
                int(self.slot_num), slot_state.pin_state, slot_state.name_state
            )
            _LOGGER.debug(...)
        else:  # active_state == STATE_OFF
            await self.lock.async_internal_clear_usercode(int(self.slot_num))
            _LOGGER.debug(...)
        self._cancel_retry()
        return True
    except LockDisconnected as err:
        _LOGGER.debug(...)
        self._schedule_retry()
        return False
```

**When Sync Operations Occur (Lines 543-612 `_async_update_state`):**
1. **Initial Load** (`_attr_is_on is None`):
   - Sets sync state WITHOUT performing operations
   - Prevents startup flapping
   - Returns early (no operations)

2. **Normal Operation** (after initial load):
   - **If out of sync** (`expected_in_sync == False`):
     - Sets `is_on = False`
     - Performs sync operation (set or clear)
     - Refreshes coordinator to verify
     - Schedules retry on failure
   - **If now in sync** (was out, now aligned):
     - Sets `is_on = True`
     - Cancels any pending retries

**Entry Point (Lines 291-310 `async_update`):**
```python
async def async_update(self) -> None:
    """Update entity."""
    if self._attr_is_on is None:
        return
    
    if (
        self._lock.locked()
        or self.is_on
        or not (state := self.hass.states.get(self.lock.lock.entity_id))
        or state.state in (STATE_UNAVAILABLE, STATE_UNKNOWN)
        or (not self.coordinator.last_update_success and not self._retry_active)
    ):
        return
    
    _LOGGER.debug(...)
    await self._async_update_state()
```
**Sync only occurs if:**
- Slot is NOT already in sync (`self.is_on == False`)
- Lock is NOT physically locked
- Lock entity is available
- Coordinator has successful updates (or retry is active)

---

### 3. PUSH UPDATE FLOW - Z-WAVE JS PROVIDER

**File:** `/Users/raman/projects/lock_code_manager/providers/zwave_js.py`

**Module Docstring (Line 1):**
```
"""Module for Z-Wave JS locks."""
```

**Push Subscription Entry Point (Lines 274-389 `subscribe_push_updates`):**
```python
@callback
def subscribe_push_updates(self) -> None:
    """Subscribe to User Code CC value update events."""
    # Idempotent - skip if already subscribed
    if self._value_update_unsub is not None:
        return
    
    ready, reason = self._get_client_state()
    if not ready:
        _LOGGER.debug(...)
        self._schedule_push_retry(reason)
        return
    
    @callback
    def on_value_updated(event: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
        """Handle value update events from Z-Wave JS."""
        # ... filters and processing ...
```

**Push Update Processing (full flow in Lines 291-389):**

1. **Filter Step:**
   - Only handle `CommandClass.USER_CODE`
   - Only handle `userCode` or `userIdStatus` properties
   - Skip slot 0 (metadata, not a user code slot)

2. **Clear In-Progress Tracking:**
   - Only on `userCode` updates for the slot being set
   - `userIdStatus` updates don't confirm (could race with duplicate rejection)

3. **Handle Status Updates** (`userIdStatus` property):
   - If status == `AVAILABLE` (slot cleared):
     - **Check:** Does LCM expect a PIN on this slot? (`_slot_expects_pin`)
     - If YES → Ignore (stale event from lock after code was set)
     - If NO → Update coordinator with empty string `""`
   - Exits early (doesn't process as code update)

4. **Handle Code Updates** (`userCode` property):
   - Normalizes `None`, empty, or all-zeros to `""`
   - Resolves masked codes (all asterisks) via `_resolve_pin_if_masked`
   - Skips duplicate events (compares with existing coordinator data)
   - **Calls:** `self.coordinator.push_update({code_slot: resolved})`

**Does NOT distinguish managed vs unmanaged slots in push handling** — processes ALL slots but may skip based on state expectations.

---

### 4. MASKED PIN RESOLUTION

**File:** `/Users/raman/projects/lock_code_manager/providers/zwave_js.py`

**`_resolve_pin_if_masked` Method (Lines 229-271):**
```python
def _resolve_pin_if_masked(self, value: str, code_slot: int) -> str | None:
    """
    Resolve a PIN value, looking up expected PIN if masked.
    
    Some locks return masked values (all asterisks) instead of the actual PIN.
    This method returns the value as-is if not masked, or looks up the expected
    PIN from LCM entities if masked.
    """
    slot_in_use = self.code_slot_in_use(code_slot)
    
    if value == "0" * len(value) and slot_in_use is False:
        # Some locks return all zeros instead of a blank value when cleared - treat
        # as unmasked cleared value
        return ""
    
    # If not masked, return as-is
    if not value or not (value == "*" * len(value) and slot_in_use):
        return value
    
    # Masked - look up expected PIN from LCM entities
    # Use ATTR_ACTIVE binary sensor (not CONF_ENABLED switch) to match what
    # sync logic expects. ATTR_ACTIVE considers conditions, so we only resolve
    # masked codes when sync expects a PIN on the lock.
    states = self._get_slot_entity_states(code_slot)
    if states is None:
        return None
    
    active_state, pin_value, pin_entity_id = states
    
    if active_state == STATE_ON:
        _LOGGER.debug(
            "PIN is masked for lock %s code slot %s, assuming value from PIN entity %s",
            self.lock.entity_id,
            code_slot,
            pin_entity_id,
        )
        return pin_value
    
    # Fall back to returning masked value if active state is not ON (e.g. slot not
    # enabled) - we don't care what the value is, just that there is one so that
    # the sync logic treats this slot as having a PIN set on the lock
    return value
```

**Key aspects:**
- Detects masks: all asterisks with `slot_in_use == True`
- Looks up expected PIN from LCM text entity (for MANAGED slots only)
- Returns `None` if slot is managed but PIN entity doesn't exist (unresolvable)
- Falls back to returning masked value for unmanaged slots (just needs non-empty)

**Helper Method (Lines 179-213 `_get_slot_entity_states`):**
```python
def _get_slot_entity_states(self, code_slot: int) -> tuple[str, str, str] | None:
    """
    Get active state, PIN value, and PIN entity ID for a managed slot.
    
    Returns tuple of (active_state, pin_value, pin_entity_id) if the slot is
    managed by LCM and entities exist, None otherwise.
    """
    try:
        config_entry = next(
            entry
            for entry in self.hass.config_entries.async_entries(DOMAIN)
            if self.lock.entity_id in get_entry_data(entry, CONF_LOCKS, [])
            and code_slot in (int(s) for s in get_entry_data(entry, CONF_SLOTS, {}))
        )
    except StopIteration:
        return None  # Not managed by any LCM entry
    
    base_unique_id = f"{config_entry.entry_id}|{code_slot}"
    active_entity_id = self.ent_reg.async_get_entity_id(
        BINARY_SENSOR_DOMAIN, DOMAIN, f"{base_unique_id}|{ATTR_ACTIVE}"
    )
    pin_entity_id = self.ent_reg.async_get_entity_id(
        TEXT_DOMAIN, DOMAIN, f"{base_unique_id}|{CONF_PIN}"
    )
    
    if not active_entity_id or not pin_entity_id:
        return None  # Entities don't exist yet
    
    active_state = self.hass.states.get(active_entity_id)
    pin_state = self.hass.states.get(pin_entity_id)
    
    if not active_state or not pin_state:
        return None
    
    return (active_state.state, pin_state.state, pin_entity_id)
```

**This IS how managed vs unmanaged is distinguished:**
- **MANAGED:** Slot exists in a config entry's `CONF_SLOTS`, and corresponding LCM entities exist
- **UNMANAGED:** Slot either not in any config entry, or entities don't exist yet

### 5. DUPLICATE CODE REACTIVE HANDLER

**File:** `/Users/raman/projects/lock_code_manager/providers/zwave_js.py`

**Entry Point (Lines 438-476 `_handle_zwave_js_event`):**
```python
@callback
def _handle_zwave_js_event(self, evt: Event) -> None:
    """Handle Z-Wave JS event."""
    if evt.data[ATTR_TYPE] != NotificationType.ACCESS_CONTROL:
        _LOGGER.debug(...)
        return
    
    params = evt.data.get(ATTR_PARAMETERS) or {}
    code_slot = params.get("userId", 0)
    
    # Handle duplicate code rejection — only when LCM initiated the set
    if (
        evt.data[ATTR_EVENT]
        == AccessControlNotificationEvent.NEW_USER_CODE_NOT_ADDED_DUE_TO_DUPLICATE_CODE
        and self._set_in_progress_code_slot is not None
        and code_slot in (0, self._set_in_progress_code_slot)
    ):
        slot = self._set_in_progress_code_slot or code_slot
        self.hass.async_create_task(
            self._async_handle_duplicate_code(),
            f"Handle duplicate code for {self.lock.entity_id} slot {slot}",
        )
        return
    
    # ... fire code slot event for other notifications ...
```

**Handler Execution (Lines 478-539 `_async_handle_duplicate_code`):**
```python
async def _async_handle_duplicate_code(self) -> None:
    """Handle duplicate code rejection from the lock."""
    lock_ent_id = self.lock.entity_id
    code_slot = self._set_in_progress_code_slot
    self._set_in_progress_code_slot = None
    if code_slot is None:
        return
    try:
        # Only move forward if a config entry manages this lock and code slot
        entry = next(
            entry
            for entry in self.hass.config_entries.async_entries(DOMAIN)
            if self.lock.entity_id in get_entry_data(entry, CONF_LOCKS, [])
            and code_slot in (int(s) for s in get_entry_data(entry, CONF_SLOTS, {}))
        )
    except StopIteration:
        return  # Not managed - ignore
    
    # Guard that will never trigger but satisfies type checkers
    if (
        enabled_ent_id := self.ent_reg.async_get_entity_id(
            SWITCH_DOMAIN, DOMAIN, f"{entry.entry_id}|{code_slot}|{CONF_ENABLED}"
        )
    ) is None:
        return
    
    # DISABLE the slot
    await self.hass.services.async_call(
        SWITCH_DOMAIN,
        SERVICE_TURN_OFF,
        {ATTR_ENTITY_ID: enabled_ent_id},
        blocking=True,
    )
    
    # Create user notification
    entry_title = entry.title or entry.entry_id
    def _dupe_msg(...) -> str:
        return (
            f"Lock {lock_entity_id} rejected the code for slot {code_slot} "
            f"(config entry {config_entry_title}) because it duplicates a code "
            f"in another slot. Slot {code_slot} has been disabled. Please set a "
            f"unique code and re-enable slot {code_slot}"
        )
    
    _LOGGER.error(_dupe_msg(...))
    async_create(
        self.hass,
        _dupe_msg(...),
        title="Duplicate Lock Code Rejected",
        notification_id=(f"{DOMAIN}_{lock_ent_id}_{code_slot}_dupe"),
    )
```

**Complete reactive flow:**
1. Z-Wave JS event listener detects `NEW_USER_CODE_NOT_ADDED_DUE_TO_DUPLICATE_CODE` notification
2. Verifies it matches `_set_in_progress_code_slot` (LCM initiated the set)
3. Checks if slot is MANAGED by a config entry
4. If managed: Disables the slot via switch entity and notifies user
5. If unmanaged: Silently ignores (lock rejected duplicate, but LCM didn't manage it)

---

### 6. SET/CLEAR OPERATIONS WITH OPTIMISTIC UPDATES

**Z-Wave JS Provider (Lines 579-627 `async_set_usercode`):**
```python
async def async_set_usercode(
    self, code_slot: int, usercode: int | str, name: str | None = None
) -> bool:
    """
    Set a usercode on a code slot.
    
    Returns True if the value was changed, False if already set to this value.
    """
    usercode = str(usercode)
    try:
        if (current := get_usercode(self.node, code_slot)).get("in_use"):
            current_code = str(current.get("usercode", ""))
            # Resolve masked codes and compare
            if usercode == self._resolve_pin_if_masked(current_code, code_slot):
                _LOGGER.debug(...)
                return False  # Already set to this value
    except Exception:
        pass  # Proceed with the set
    
    self._set_in_progress_code_slot = code_slot
    service_data = {
        ATTR_ENTITY_ID: self.lock.entity_id,
        ATTR_CODE_SLOT: code_slot,
        ATTR_USERCODE: usercode,
    }
    await self.async_call_service(
        ZWAVE_JS_DOMAIN, SERVICE_SET_LOCK_USERCODE, service_data
    )
    # V1 locks don't reliably update cache after set - poll directly
    if self._usercode_cc_version < 2:
        await get_usercode_from_node(self.node, code_slot)
    # Optimistic update: command succeeded, update coordinator immediately
    if self.coordinator:
        self.coordinator.push_update({code_slot: usercode})
    return True
```

**Clear Operation (Lines 629-668 `async_clear_usercode`):**
```python
async def async_clear_usercode(self, code_slot: int) -> bool:
    """
    Clear a usercode on a code slot.
    
    Returns True if the value was changed, False if already cleared.
    """
    try:
        current = get_usercode(self.node, code_slot)
        if not current.get("in_use"):
            _LOGGER.debug(...)
            return False  # Already cleared
    except Exception:
        pass
    
    service_data = {
        ATTR_ENTITY_ID: self.lock.entity_id,
        ATTR_CODE_SLOT: code_slot,
    }
    await self.async_call_service(
        ZWAVE_JS_DOMAIN, SERVICE_CLEAR_LOCK_USERCODE, service_data
    )
    # V1 locks need direct poll for cache update
    if self._usercode_cc_version < 2:
        await get_usercode_from_node(self.node, code_slot)
    # Optimistic update
    if self.coordinator:
        self.coordinator.push_update({code_slot: ""})
    return True
```

**Key flow:**
1. Check cache if code is already set/cleared (skip if no change)
2. Track slot in `_set_in_progress_code_slot` (for duplicate detection)
3. Call Z-Wave service
4. V1 locks: Force cache poll from device
5. Z-Wave JS: Optimistically update coordinator via `push_update`
   - Push-based providers do this to avoid sync loops from stale cache
   - Non-push providers refresh coordinator after, reading from cache

**Base Wrapper (Lines 534-548 `async_internal_set_usercode`):**
```python
@final
async def async_internal_set_usercode(
    self, code_slot: int, usercode: int | str, name: str | None = None
) -> None:
    """Set a usercode on a code slot."""
    changed = await self._execute_rate_limited(
        "set", self.async_set_usercode, code_slot, usercode, name=name
    )
    # Refresh coordinator to update entity states from cache (only if changed).
    # Skip for push-based providers — they update the coordinator optimistically
    # via push_update() in their set/clear methods, and refreshing from cache
    # could overwrite the optimistic update with stale data when the underlying
    # driver defers cache updates until device confirmation.
    if changed and self.coordinator and not self.supports_push:
        await self.coordinator.async_request_refresh()
```

---

### 7. RATE LIMITING & SERIALIZATION

**Base Provider (Lines 139-172 `_execute_rate_limited`):**
```python
async def _execute_rate_limited(
    self,
    operation_type: Literal["get", "set", "clear", "refresh"],
    func: Callable[..., Awaitable[Any]],
    *args: Any,
    **kwargs: Any,
) -> Any:
    """Execute operation with connection check, serialization, and delay."""
    if not await self.async_internal_is_connection_up():
        raise LockDisconnected(
            f"Cannot {_OPERATION_MESSAGES[operation_type]} {self.lock.entity_id} - lock not connected"
        )
    
    async with self._aio_lock:  # Serializes ALL lock operations
        elapsed = time.monotonic() - self._last_operation_time
        if elapsed < self._min_operation_delay:
            delay = self._min_operation_delay - elapsed
            LOGGER.debug(...)
            await asyncio.sleep(delay)
        
        LOGGER.debug(...)
        result = await func(*args, **kwargs)
        self._last_operation_time = time.monotonic()
        return result
```

**Rate Limiting Constants:** 2-second minimum delay between lock operations (defined as `MIN_OPERATION_DELAY` in _base.py)

### 8. INITIALIZATION CHAIN

**Entry:** `/Users/raman/projects/lock_code_manager/providers/_base.py` Lines 308-356 `_async_setup_internal`

```python
async def _async_setup_internal(self, config_entry: ConfigEntry) -> None:
    """Set up lock and coordinator."""
    lock_entity_id = self.lock.entity_id
    # Track the provider's config entry state
    self._setup_config_entry_state_listener()
    
    # Reuse existing coordinator or create new one
    if self.coordinator is not None:
        self.hass.async_create_task(
            self.coordinator.async_request_refresh(),
            f"Refresh coordinator for {lock_entity_id}",
        )
        return
    
    self.coordinator = LockUsercodeUpdateCoordinator(self.hass, self, config_entry)
    if config_entry.state == ConfigEntryState.SETUP_IN_PROGRESS:
        try:
            await self.coordinator.async_config_entry_first_refresh()
        except (ConfigEntryNotReady, UpdateFailed) as err:
            LOGGER.warning(...)
    else:
        await self.coordinator.async_refresh()
        if not self.coordinator.last_update_success:
            LOGGER.warning(...)
    
    # Subscribe to push updates after coordinator is ready
    if self.supports_push:
        if (
            self.lock_config_entry
            and self.lock_config_entry.state != ConfigEntryState.LOADED
        ):
            LOGGER.debug(...)
        else:
            self.subscribe_push_updates()
```

---

### 9. MANAGED VS UNMANAGED DISTINCTION

**KEY FINDING:** The codebase does NOT distinguish managed vs unmanaged at the coordinator level. The distinction is made at the PUSH UPDATE and MASKED PIN RESOLUTION level:

**Managed Slot:** 
- Exists in a config entry's `CONF_SLOTS` dictionary
- Has corresponding LCM binary sensor (ATTR_ACTIVE) and text entity (CONF_PIN)
- When masked PIN is received, it's resolved to expected PIN from text entity
- When duplicate code notification occurs, slot is disabled via switch entity

**Unmanaged Slot:**
- Does not exist in any LCM config entry's slots
- Or exists but entities haven't been created yet
- Masked values are kept as-is (or return `None`)
- Duplicate code notifications are silently ignored

**Detection Method:** `_get_slot_entity_states()` (Lines 179-213 in zwave_js.py)
- Searches all LCM config entries for one managing this lock and slot
- Returns `None` if not found or entities don't exist → unmanaged

> AGENT

Good. Now I have the complete picture. Key findings that shape the design:

1. **Coordinator stores ALL slots** (managed + unmanaged), no distinction
2. **Masked PINs are a complication** — for unmanaged slots with masked codes, we can't know the actual PIN, so we can't reliably detect duplicates against them
3. **Module docstrings are minimal** — just one-liners like `"Module for Z-Wave JS locks."`
4. **The managed/unmanaged distinction is implicit** — scattered across `_get_slot_entity_states`, push update filters, and sync logic

First clarifying question:

For the duplicate detection — when a lock masks PINs (returns `****` instead of the actual code), we can resolve managed slots via the text entity, but **unmanaged slots with masked PINs are opaque**. In that case we can't detect duplicates against them pre-flight. 

Should we:
- **(A)** Only pre-check against slots where we know the actual PIN (managed + unmanaged-with-visible-codes), and rely on the Z-Wave event handler as a fallback for masked unmanaged slots
- **(B)** Treat masked unmanaged slots as a warning condition — flag to the user that duplicate detection is incomplete because some slots have unknown PINs
- **(C)** Something else?

> DEVELOPER

A is the best option available. If there are other options to explore for C, can we do that?

> AGENT

A few ideas for C:

1. **Clear unmanaged slots proactively** — The UI already shows unmanaged slots with edit/clear capability. We could add a "Clear all unmanaged codes" action (button in the lock-codes card or a service call). This eliminates the duplicate risk at the source rather than detecting it. Users with stale codes from old systems would benefit most.

2. **Cache PINs before they get masked** — Some locks only mask on *subsequent* reads after the initial set. If we captured the raw PIN on the first push update and stored it in coordinator data alongside the masked value, we'd have visibility into unmanaged slots too. But this is unreliable — it depends on lock firmware behavior and we'd miss codes set before LCM was installed.

3. **Probe via set-and-check** — Before setting the real code, attempt to set a known dummy code on the target slot. If it succeeds, the slot is writable and we can then set the real code. This doesn't help with duplicate detection though, so it's not useful here.

Honestly, I think **(1)** is the only one worth pursuing alongside A. Options 2 and 3 are fragile. And (1) is more of a UX enhancement than a detection mechanism — it's solving "how do users clean up their locks" rather than "how do we detect duplicates."

So my recommendation: **A as the core approach** (pre-check all visible PINs, event handler as fallback for masked/unknown), with **(1) as a separate future enhancement** if users keep hitting this.

Does that sound right, or do you want to dig deeper into any of these?

> DEVELOPER

I agree A and 1 as a future enhancement. For this enhancement, we should update the config flow text to make it clear that some locks store PINs as masked, and consuming applications can only see it that way. Therefore, the most reliable way for this to work is to ensure that you only manage codes through a tool like LCM or Keymaster (choose 1 but not both). It's highly recommended to clear all slots before setting LCM up, even ones that were already set. If you need permanent access, just configure the slot through LCM without a condition. Something to that effect but less cerbose

OK, here's the design. I'll present it in sections.

---

## Section 1: Pre-flight Duplicate Detection

**Where:** `async_set_usercode()` in `zwave_js.py` (line ~612, before the service call)

**Logic:** Before calling the Z-Wave set service, scan `self.coordinator.data` for any other slot with the same PIN value. Skip slots where the value is masked (all `*`) since we can't compare. Skip the target slot itself.

**On duplicate found:**
- Don't call the Z-Wave service at all
- Disable the slot (same as current event handler)
- Create a persistent notification, but with a **better message**: identify *which* slot has the conflicting code and whether it's managed or unmanaged
- Log an error

**Masked slots:** If there are unmanaged slots with masked PINs, we can't check them — fall through to the existing event 15 handler as the safety net.

**Scope:** This lives in the Z-Wave provider since duplicate rejection is a lock firmware behavior, not universal. Other providers can add their own pre-flight checks if needed.

## Section 1 (revised): Pre-flight Duplicate Detection

**Where:** `async_set_usercode()` in `zwave_js.py`, before the Z-Wave service call.

**Logic:** Scan `self.coordinator.data` for any other slot with the same PIN. Skip masked values (`*`), skip the target slot.

**Two behaviors depending on caller:**

- **Sync path** (`_perform_sync_operation` → `async_internal_set_usercode`): Disable the slot + persistent notification identifying the conflicting slot and whether it's managed/unmanaged. Same spirit as current event 15 handler but with better info and no round-trip to the lock.

- **Direct path** (user calls `text.set_value` or websocket): Raise an error / fail the action. The user set the PIN explicitly, so they should get immediate feedback, not have their slot silently disabled.

**Implementation:** `async_set_usercode` needs to know which path called it. Options:
- **(A)** Add a parameter like `disable_on_duplicate: bool = True` — sync passes `True`, direct sets pass `False`
- **(B)** Raise a specific exception (`DuplicateCodeError`) and let the caller decide — sync catches it and disables, direct path lets it propagate

I'd recommend **(B)** — it keeps `async_set_usercode` focused on detection/raising, and pushes the policy (disable vs fail) to the callers who know the context.

**Masked slots fallback:** Event 15 handler stays as safety net.

## Section 3: Architecture Documentation

Currently every module docstring is a one-liner (`"Module for Z-Wave JS locks."`, `"Lock Code Manager Coordinators."`, etc.). The managed vs unmanaged distinction, the push/poll/refresh model, and the data flow between coordinator → sync sensor → provider are all implicit — you have to read the code to piece it together.

**Two approaches:**

**(A) Module docstrings** — Expand the one-liners in the key files (`zwave_js.py`, `coordinator.py`, `binary_sensor.py`, `_base.py`) with a paragraph each covering that module's role in the data flow.

**(B) Architecture doc in the codebase** — A single `ARCHITECTURE.md` (or section in `AGENTS.md`) that describes the system-level flow: what gets polled vs pushed, how sync decisions work, managed vs unmanaged slot visibility, the role of each module. Individual module docstrings stay lean and point to this doc.

**My recommendation: B.** The data flow is cross-cutting — it spans coordinator, provider, and sync sensor. Splitting it across module docstrings means you have to read 4 files to get the full picture, and the pieces can drift out of sync. One authoritative doc keeps it coherent and is easier to maintain.

The module docstrings would get a small upgrade — 2-3 sentences describing the module's role + a pointer to the architecture doc for the full picture.

That's the full design. To summarize what's **in scope**:

1. **`DuplicateCodeError`** in `async_set_usercode` — pre-flight check against all visible PINs in coordinator data
2. **Sync path** catches it → disables slot + persistent notification with conflicting slot details
3. **Direct path** lets it propagate → action fails with clear error
4. **Event 15 handler stays** as safety net for masked/unknown slots
5. **Config flow text** — `choose_path` and `num_slots` rewording
6. **Architecture doc** — single `ARCHITECTURE.md` covering data flow, push/poll/refresh, managed vs unmanaged
7. **Module docstrings** — brief role description + pointer to architecture doc

    73→#### Move Sync Logic to Coordinator
    74→
    75→**Current Architecture Issues:**
    76→
    77→- In-sync binary sensor reads PIN config from text entities via
    78→  `_get_entity_state()`
    79→- Reads lock state from coordinator data
    80→- Compares them and triggers sync operations (set_usercode/clear_usercode)
    81→- Cross-entity dependencies create race conditions during startup
    82→- Current guard uses `_attr_is_on is None` to avoid initial sync operations
    83→
    84→**Proposed Solution:**
    85→Move sync logic entirely into the coordinator:
    86→
    87→- Coordinator already has access to both desired state (from config) and actual
    88→  state (from lock)
    89→- Coordinator performs sync operations during its `_async_update_data()` cycle
    90→- Binary sensor becomes read-only, just displays coordinator's computed in-sync
    91→  status
    92→- Text/number/switch entities remain as config views
    93→
    94→**Example Implementation:**
    95→
    96→```python
    97→# In coordinator._async_update_data()
    98→actual_code = await self.provider.get_usercodes()
    99→desired_code = self.config_entry.data[slot]["pin"]
   100→
   101→if actual_code != desired_code and slot_enabled:
   102→    await self.provider.set_usercode(slot, desired_code)
   103→
   104→return {"in_sync": actual_code == desired_code, "actual_code": actual_code}
   105→```
   106→
   107→**Benefits:**
   108→
   109→- Eliminates cross-entity state reading
   110→- Removes `_initial_state_loaded` flag and startup detection logic
   111→- No race conditions during startup
   112→- Simpler, more centralized sync logic
   113→- Coordinator is single source of truth
   114→
   115→**Considerations:**
   116→
   117→- Major architectural change
   118→- Would need to update binary sensor to be read-only
   119→- Config updates still flow through text/switch entities
   120→- Need to ensure coordinator runs sync on config changes
   121→
   122→**Estimated Effort:** High (16-24 hours)
   123→**Priority:** Medium
   124→**Status:** Not started
   125→
   126→### Convert Config and Internal Dicts to Dataclasses
   127→
   128→Convert config entry data to typed dataclasses with `from_dict`/`from_entry`
   129→class methods. Use object instances internally instead of iterating through raw
   130→config dicts. Audit codebase for other complex dicts that would benefit from
   131→dataclass conversion (e.g., slot data, lock state, coordinator data). This
   132→improves type safety, IDE autocompletion, and code readability.
   133→
   134→**Why not Voluptuous?** Voluptuous is for validation, not object instantiation.
   135→Other options like `dacite` or Pydantic add dependencies.
   136→
   137→**Example implementation:**
   138→
   139→```python
   140→@dataclass
   141→class SlotConfig:
   142→    name: str
   143→    pin: str
   144→    enabled: bool = True
   145→    calendar: str | None = None
   146→    number_of_uses: int | None = None
   147→
   148→    @classmethod
   149→    def from_dict(cls, data: dict) -> SlotConfig:
   150→        return cls(
   151→            name=data[CONF_NAME],
   152→            pin=data[CONF_PIN],
   153→            enabled=data.get(CONF_ENABLED, True),
   154→            calendar=data.get(CONF_CALENDAR),
   155→            number_of_uses=data.get(CONF_NUMBER_OF_USES),
   156→        )
   157→
   158→@dataclass
   159→class LCMConfig:
   160→    locks: list[str]
   161→    slots: dict[int, SlotConfig]
   162→
   163→    @classmethod
   164→    def from_entry(cls, entry: ConfigEntry) -> LCMConfig:
   165→        return cls(
   166→            locks=get_entry_data(entry, CONF_LOCKS, []),
   167→            slots={
   168→                int(k): SlotConfig.from_dict(v)
   169→                for k, v in get_entry_data(entry, CONF_SLOTS, {}).items()
   170→            },
   171→        )
   172→```
   173→
   174→**Places to audit for dict-to-dataclass conversion:**
   175→
   176→- `config_entry.data` / `config_entry.options` access patterns
   177→- `get_entry_data()` / `get_slot_data()` return values
   178→- Coordinator `self.data` structure
   179→- Lock provider internal state
   180→
   181→### Handle Disabled Lock Slots
   182→
   183→**Context:** Z-Wave locks have `userIdStatus` which can be `Enabled`, `Available`,
   184→or `Disabled`. Currently the coordinator only stores the code value, not the status.
   185→
   186→**Investigation Needed:**
   187→
   188→1. Test what happens when LCM tries to set a user code on a slot with
   189→   `userIdStatus=Disabled`
   190→2. Determine if we need to explicitly enable the slot before setting a code
   191→3. Check if different lock brands handle disabled slots differently
   192→
   193→**Related:** See "Enhance Coordinator Data Model" below.
   194→
   195→**Estimated Effort:** Medium (4-8 hours)
   196→**Priority:** Medium
   197→**Status:** Not started
   198→
   199→### Enhance Coordinator Data Model with Slot Status
   200→
   201→**Current State:** Coordinator stores `{slot: code}` mapping only.
   202→
   203→**Proposed Change:** Store `{slot: {code, status}}` where status is a generic
   204→LCM enum that providers map to.
   205→
   206→**Generic Status Enum:**
   207→
   208→```python
   209→class SlotStatus(StrEnum):
   210→    ENABLED = "enabled"    # Slot has active code
   211→    AVAILABLE = "available"  # Slot can be used but is empty
   212→    DISABLED = "disabled"  # Slot cannot be used (locked out)
   213→```
   214→
   215→**Provider Mapping:**
   216→
   217→- Z-Wave JS: Maps `userIdStatus` (Enabled/Available/Disabled) → `SlotStatus`
   218→- Other providers: Map their equivalent states to the generic enum
   219→
   220→**Benefits:**
   221→
   222→- Frontend can distinguish between "slot is empty" vs "slot is disabled"
   223→- Better handling of disabled slots in sync logic
   224→- More accurate representation of lock state
   225→- Provider-agnostic data model
   226→
   227→**Implementation:**
   228→
   229→1. Define `SlotStatus` enum in `const.py`
   230→2. Update Z-Wave JS provider to track userIdStatus and map to `SlotStatus`
   231→   (currently filtered out in `on_value_updated`)
   232→3. Change coordinator data schema from `dict[int, str]` to
   233→   `dict[int, SlotData]` where `SlotData` includes code and status
   234→4. Update all consumers of coordinator data (binary_sensor, sensor, websocket)
   235→5. Update frontend types and rendering
   236→
   237→**Estimated Effort:** High (12-16 hours)
   238→**Priority:** Medium
   239→**Status:** Not started
   240→
   241→### Add Optional Flags to `get_config_entry_data` Websocket Command
   242→
   243→**Context:** PR #787 consolidated multiple websocket commands into a single
   244→`get_config_entry_data` command that returns `{config_entry, entities, locks, slots}`.
   245→However, not all callers need all the data:
   246→
   247→| Caller | config_entry | entities | locks | slots |
   248→| ------ | ------------ | -------- | ----- | ----- |
   249→| `view-strategy.ts` | ✅ | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ |
   250→| `generate-view.ts` | ❌ | ❌ | ✅ | ✅ |
   251→| `slot-section-strategy.ts` (legacy) | ✅ | ✅ | ❌ | ✅ |
   252→| `dashboard-strategy.ts` | ❌ | ❌ | ✅ | ❌ |
   253→| `lock-codes-card-editor.ts` | ❌ | ❌ | ✅ | ❌ |
   254→
   255→**Cost Analysis:**
   256→
   257→- `config_entry` - Very cheap (just `config_entry.as_json_fragment`)
   258→- `slots` - Very cheap (extracts calendar IDs from config)
   259→- `locks` - Moderate (iterates locks, gets friendly names via state lookups)
   260→- `entities` - Most expensive (queries entity registry, maps to `as_partial_dict`)
   261→
   262→**Proposed Change:** Add optional `include_entities` and `include_locks` flags
   263→(default `True` for backwards compatibility). Skip `config_entry` and `slots`
   264→flags since they're essentially free.
   265→
   266→```python
   267→vol.Optional("include_entities", default=True): bool,
   268→vol.Optional("include_locks", default=True): bool,
   269→```
   270→
   271→**Benefits:**
   272→
   273→- `view-strategy.ts` can skip both entities and locks
   274→- `dashboard-strategy.ts` and `lock-codes-card-editor.ts` can skip entities
   275→- Legacy `slot-section-strategy.ts` can skip locks
   276→
   277→**Estimated Effort:** Low (2-4 hours)
   278→**Priority:** Low
   279→**Status:** Not started
   280→
   281→### Entity Registry Change Detection
   282→
   283→Track entity registry updates and warn if LCM entities change entity IDs (reload
   284→required).
   285→
   286→### Add Provider Diagnostic Data Method
   287→
   288→Add `get_diagnostic_data()` method to `BaseLock` for exposing provider-specific
   289→diagnostic information. Keymaster has this pattern with `get_platform_data()`.
   290→
   291→**Example Implementation:**
   292→
   293→```python
   294→# In BaseLock
   295→def get_diagnostic_data(self) -> dict[str, Any]:
   296→    """Return provider-specific diagnostic data."""
   297→    return {}
   298→
   299→# In ZWaveJSLock
   300→def get_diagnostic_data(self) -> dict[str, Any]:
   301→    return {
   302→        "node_id": self.node.node_id,
   303→        "home_id": self.node.client.driver.controller.home_id,
   304→        "client_connected": self.node.client.connected,
   305→        "config_entry_state": self.lock_config_entry.state.value,
   306→    }
   307→```
   308→
   309→**Benefits:**
   310→
   311→- Easier troubleshooting of provider-specific issues
   312→- Can be exposed via diagnostics platform or websocket
   313→
   314→**Estimated Effort:** Low (2-4 hours)
   315→**Priority:** Low
   316→**Status:** Not started
   317→
   318→### Drift Detection Failure Alerting
   319→
   320→Add mechanism to alert users when drift detection consistently fails over
   321→extended periods (e.g., lock offline). Currently failures are logged but there is
   322→no visibility to users or entities.
   323→
   324→## Features
   325→
   326→### Add Schedule and Entity Condition Types
   327→
   328→**Problem:** Users report that managing calendars in HA is too painful for simple
   329→recurring schedules. Calendars are overkill for "weekdays 9-5" patterns.
   330→
   331→**Proposed Solution:** Add alternative condition types alongside `calendar`:
   332→
   333→| Condition Type | Use Case |
   334→| -------------- | -------- |
   335→| `calendar` | One-time events, external calendar sync (existing) |
   336→| `schedule` | Recurring weekly patterns via HA schedule helper |
   337→| `entity` | Custom logic via any binary sensor/input_boolean |
   338→
   339→**Implementation:**
   340→
   341→1. **Config Flow Changes:**
   342→   - Add condition type selector (calendar/schedule/entity)
   343→   - Show appropriate entity selector based on type
   344→   - `schedule`: EntitySelector filtered to `schedule` domain
   345→   - `entity`: EntitySelector filtered to `binary_sensor`, `input_boolean`
   346→
   347→2. **Binary Sensor Changes:**
   348→   - Update `_get_entity_state()` to handle different condition types
   349→   - Schedule entities: check if current time is within schedule (state = "on")
   350→   - Entity conditions: directly use entity state
   351→
   352→3. **Slot Data Model:**
   353→   - Add `condition_type` field: `calendar | schedule | entity`
   354→   - Add `condition_entity` field (alternative to `calendar`)
   355→   - Maintain backward compatibility with existing `calendar` field
   356→
   357→4. **Frontend Updates:**
   358→   - Update slot card to show condition type
   359→   - Show appropriate icon/label for each type
   360→
   361→**Benefits:**
   362→
   363→- Schedule helper is much simpler for recurring patterns
   364→- Entity condition allows maximum flexibility (templates, automations)
   365→- Calendar remains available for complex/one-time events
   366→
   367→**Estimated Effort:** Medium (8-12 hours)
   368→**Priority:** Medium-High
   369→**Status:** Not started
   370→
   371→### Advanced Calendar Configuration
   372→
   373→**Feature Request:** Allow slot number and PIN to be configured directly in
   374→calendar event metadata, eliminating need for separate slot configuration.
   375→
   376→**Design Considerations:**
   377→
   378→**Current Behavior:**
   379→
   380→- Slot configured in config flow with fixed PIN
   381→- Calendar event (when present) only controls whether slot is active/inactive
   382→- PIN and slot number are static configuration
   383→
   384→**Proposed Behavior:**
   385→
   386→- Calendar event contains slot number and PIN in its metadata
   387→- User configures regex/pattern to extract slot number and PIN from:
   388→  - Event title (e.g., "Slot 3: 1234")
   389→  - Event description
   390→  - Event location
   391→  - Custom calendar properties
   392→
   393→**Implementation Requirements:**
   394→
   395→1. **Config Flow Changes:**
   396→   - Add "advanced calendar mode" toggle per slot
   397→   - When enabled, show pattern configuration UI
   398→   - Pattern fields: slot number regex, PIN regex, which field to parse
   399→     (title/description/location)
   400→2. **Entity Changes:**
   401→   - Calendar event listener needs to parse event metadata
   402→   - Extract slot number and PIN based on user-defined patterns
   403→   - Validate extracted values (numeric slot, PIN format)
   404→3. **Binary Sensor Changes:**
   405→   - Check if calendar event contains valid extracted values
   406→   - Use extracted PIN instead of configured PIN
   407→   - Handle multiple calendar events with different slots
   408→
   409→**Example Patterns:**
   410→
   411→- Title: "Guest Access: Slot 5, PIN 9876" -> Slot: `\d+`, PIN: `\d{4}$`
   412→- Description: "Code: 1234 for slot 3" -> Slot: `slot (\d+)`, PIN: `Code: (\d+)`
   413→- Location: "5:1234" -> Slot: `^(\d+):`, PIN: `:(\d+)$`
   414→
   415→**Challenges:**
   416→
   417→- Multiple calendar events with different slots
   418→- Error handling for invalid patterns
   419→- UI for testing patterns
   420→- Backward compatibility with existing simple calendar mode
   421→
   422→**Estimated Effort:** Very High (40+ hours)
   423→**Priority:** Medium
   424→**Status:** Not started
   425→
   426→### Add Relevant New Home Assistant Core Features
   427→
   428→**Analysis:** Review Home Assistant release notes from 2024.1 through current
   429→and integrate relevant new features.
   430→
   431→**Key Features to Evaluate (2024-2025):**
   432→
   433→1. **Entity Category Enhancements** (2024.x)
   434→   - New entity categories available
   435→   - **Action:** Review entity category assignments
   436→2. **Selector Improvements** (2024.x)
   437→   - New selector types for config flow
   438→   - **Action:** Review config flow UI for better selectors
   439→3. **Repair Platform** (2024.x)
   440→   - Notify users of configuration issues
   441→   - **Action:** Consider adding repairs for common misconfigurations
   442→
   443→**Already Evaluated (No Changes Needed):**
   444→
   445→- **Config Entry Runtime Data**: `hass.data[DOMAIN]` correctly holds global
   446→  cross-config-entry state (lock registry, resource flag). Per-entry data
   447→  already uses `runtime_data`.
   448→- **DataUpdateCoordinator `_async_setup()`**: All coordinator setup is
   449→  synchronous (timer registration). No async initialization needed.
   450→- **Config Entry Diagnostics**: No significant internal state to expose beyond
   451→  what's already visible via entities and config entries.
   452→
   453→**Estimated Effort:** Low-Medium (4-8 hours)
   454→**Priority:** Medium
   455→**Status:** Partially evaluated
   456→
   457→### Add Support for Additional Lock Providers
   458→
   459→**Current Providers:**
   460→
   461→- Z-Wave JS (`zwave_js.py`)
   462→- Virtual (`virtual.py`) - for testing only
   463→
   464→**Available Lock Integrations in Home Assistant Core:**
   465→
   466→**Smart Home Platforms:**
   467→
   468→- `deconz` - deCONZ (Zigbee/Z-Wave gateway)
   469→- `homematic` - Homematic (CCU)
   470→- `homematicip_cloud` - Homematic IP Cloud
   471→- `homekit_controller` - HomeKit accessories
   472→- `matter` - Matter protocol (PR open)
   473→- `mqtt` - MQTT locks
   474→- `smartthings` - SmartThings
   475→- `zha` - Zigbee Home Automation
   476→- `zwave_js` - Z-Wave JS (already supported)
   477→- `zwave_me` - Z-Wave.Me
   478→
   479→**Brand-Specific Integrations:**
   480→
   481→- `abode` - Abode Security
   482→- `bmw_connected_drive` - BMW Connected Drive
   483→- `dormakaba_dkey` - Dormakaba dkey
   484→- `igloohome` - igloohome
   485→- `kiwi` - Kiwi (Eufy)
   486→- `loqed` - Loqed Smart Lock
   487→- `nuki` - Nuki Smart Lock
   488→- `schlage` - Schlage Encode
   489→- `sesame` - Sesame Smart Lock
   490→- `switchbot` - SwitchBot Lock
   491→- `switchbot_cloud` - SwitchBot Cloud
   492→- `tedee` - Tedee Smart Lock
   493→- `yolink` - YoLink
   494→
   495→**Security Systems:**
   496→
   497→- `simplisafe` - SimpliSafe
   498→- `verisure` - Verisure
   499→
   500→**Cannot Be Supported** (see README for details):
   501→
   502→- `esphome` - No user code API in ESPHome
   503→- `august`, `yale`, `yalexs_ble`, `yale_smart_alarm` - Library limitations
   504→
   505→**Vehicle Integrations:**
   506→
   507→- `subaru` - Subaru Starlink
   508→- `tesla_fleet` - Tesla Fleet API
   509→- `teslemetry` - Teslemetry
   510→- `tessie` - Tessie (Tesla)
   511→- `starline` - StarLine
   512→
   513→**Other Integrations:**
   514→
   515→- `fibaro` - Fibaro
   516→- `freedompro` - Freedompro
   517→- `insteon` - Insteon
   518→- `isy994` - Universal Devices ISY
   519→- `keba` - KEBA EV Charger
   520→- `overkiz` - Overkiz (Somfy TaHoma)
   521→- `surepetcare` - Sure Petcare
   522→- `unifiprotect` - UniFi Protect
   523→- `vera` - Vera
   524→- `wallbox` - Wallbox EV Charger
   525→- `xiaomi_aqara` - Xiaomi Aqara
   526→
   527→**Utility/Helper Integrations:**
   528→
   529→- `demo` - Demo platform
   530→- `group` - Lock groups
   531→- `kitchen_sink` - Testing platform
   532→- `switch_as_x` - Convert switches to locks
   533→- `template` - Template locks
   534→- `homee` - Homee gateway
   535→
   536→**Recommended Priorities for Support:**
   537→
   538→**High Priority** (popular, widely used):
   539→
   540→1. **ZHA (Zigbee Home Automation)** - Very popular, supports many lock brands
   541→2. **Matter** - Future-proof, industry standard (PR open)
   542→3. **MQTT** - Generic protocol, many custom implementations
   543→
   544→**Medium Priority** (brand-specific, popular):
   545→
   546→1. **Nuki** - Popular in Europe
   547→2. **Schlage** - Popular in North America
   548→3. **SwitchBot** - Growing popularity
   549→
   550→**Low Priority** (niche or less common):
   551→
   552→- Vehicle locks (Tesla, BMW, Subaru) - different use case
   553→- Security system locks - usually managed by their own systems
   554→- Utility integrations (template, group) - may work without specific provider
   555→
   556→**Implementation Approach:**
   557→
   558→For each new provider:
   559→
   560→1. Create `providers/INTEGRATION_NAME.py`
   561→2. Subclass `BaseLock`
   562→3. Implement required methods
   563→4. Add integration-specific event listeners
   564→5. Add tests in `tests/INTEGRATION_NAME/test_provider.py`
   565→6. Update documentation
   566→
   567→**Estimated Effort per Provider:** Medium (6-12 hours each)
   568→**Priority:** Medium-High
   569→**Status:** Not started
   570→**Recommended First Addition:** ZHA (Zigbee)
   571→
   572→### Improve Dashboard UI/UX
   573→

   594→- `lock_code_manager.bulk_disable` - Disable multiple slots at once
   595→- `lock_code_manager.copy_slot` - Copy configuration from one slot to another
   596→- Note: `hard_refresh_usercodes` service already exists for lock-wide refresh.

   598→**Estimated Effort:** Medium (8-16 hours)

   616→### Configuration Validation
   617→
   618→**Ideas:**
   619→

   624→
   625→**Estimated Effort:** Low-Medium (4-8 hours)
   626→**Priority:** Medium
   627→**Status:** Not started
   628→
   629→### Additional Feature Notes
   630→
   631→- Better out-of-sync visibility in the UI.
   632→- Websocket commands expose lock data via `lock_code_manager/subscribe_lock_codes`
   633→  and slot data via `lock_code_manager/subscribe_code_slot`. The `lcm-lock-codes`

{
  "file_path": "/Users/raman/projects/lock_code_manager/docs/plans/2026-03-28-duplicate-code-detection-design.md",
  "content": "# Duplicate Code Detection + Architecture Documentation\n\n**Date:** 2026-03-28\n**Status:** Approved\n\n## Problem\n\nSome locks reject setting a PIN if the same code exists in another slot. When this\nhappens, the Z-Wave service call succeeds but the lock silently rejects it. LCM sees\nthe code isn't set, retries, and loops indefinitely.\n\nPR #899 added reactive handling via Z-Wave event 15\n(`NEW_USER_CODE_NOT_ADDED_DUE_TO_DUPLICATE_CODE`), but users still report infinite\nsync loops — likely because not all locks emit this event reliably.\n\nAdditionally, the codebase lacks clarity on:\n- Which slots LCM sees vs manages\n- How data flows between coordinator, provider, and sync sensor\n- The managed vs unmanaged distinction (implicit, scattered across modules)\n\n## Design\n\n### 1. Pre-flight Duplicate Detection\n\n**Where:** `async_set_usercode()` in `zwave_js.py`, before the Z-Wave service call.\n\n**Logic:** Scan `self.coordinator.data` for any other slot with the same PIN value.\nSkip masked values (all `*`). Skip the target slot.\n\n**New exception:** `DuplicateCodeError` — raised by `async_set_usercode()` when a\nduplicate is found. Includes the conflicting slot number and whether it's managed or\nunmanaged.\n\n**Caller-specific handling:**\n\n- **Sync path** (`_perform_sync_operation`): Catches `DuplicateCodeError`, disables\n  the slot via the enabled switch, creates a persistent notification identifying the\n  conflicting slot.\n- **Direct path** (`text.set_value`, websocket): Lets the exception propagate — the\n  action fails with a clear error message. The user explicitly set the PIN, so they\n  should get immediate feedback, not have their slot silently disabled.\n\n**Masked slots:** For unmanaged slots with masked PINs, we can't know the actual\ncode. The existing event 15 handler stays as a safety net for these cases. The two\napproaches are complementary.\n\n### 2. Config Flow Text Updates\n\n**`choose_path` step description:**\n\n> \"Some locks mask stored PINs, making them invisible to external tools. Once LCM is\n> managing your locks, do not set codes using other tools or manually at the lock.\n> Clear all slots before setup. For permanent access codes, configure them through LCM\n> without a condition entity.\"\n\n**`num_slots` step description:**\n\n> \"Configure slots for all codes you want on the lock (existing user codes for the\n> slot range specified will be cleared). You can add and remove slots later using the\n> integration services.\"\n\n### 3. Architecture Documentation\n\n**New file:** `ARCHITECTURE.md` in the repo root.\n\nCovers the system-level data flow:\n- What gets polled vs pushed vs hard-refreshed\n- Coordinator data model (all slots, managed + unmanaged)\n- Sync decision flow (binary sensor)\n- Managed vs unmanaged slot distinction\n- Push update processing\n- Masked PIN resolution\n- Rate limiting and serialization\n\n**Module docstrings:** Upgrade from one-liners to 2-3 sentences describing the\nmodule's role + pointer to `ARCHITECTURE.md`. Files: `coordinator.py`,\n`binary_sensor.py`, `zwave_js.py`, `_base.py`.\n\n### 4. Event 15 Handler (Keep As-Is)\n\nThe reactive handler from PR #899 stays. It covers:\n- Locks that don't have their PINs in the coordinator (edge cases)\n- Race conditions (code set at keypad between read and write)\n- Unmanaged slots with masked PINs\n\n## Out of Scope (Future TODOs)\n\nCaptured in `TODO.md`:\n\n1. **Config flow lock reset step** — Check for unmanaged codes, require reset before\n   continuing\n2. **Conflicting integration detection** — Warn if Keymaster etc. is installed\n3. **Update migration docs** — Remove \"slowly migrate\" guidance\n4. **\"Clear all unmanaged\" UI action** — Bulk cleanup of stale codes\n5. **Lock-specific carve-outs** — Adapt detection per lock capability\n\n## Files to Modify\n\n| File | Change |\n|------|--------|\n| `providers/zwave_js.py` | Add duplicate check in `async_set_usercode()` |\n| `exceptions.py` (or new) | Add `DuplicateCodeError` |\n| `binary_sensor.py` | Catch `DuplicateCodeError` in sync path, disable + notify |\n| `text.py` | Let `DuplicateCodeError` propagate from set action |\n| `strings.json` | Update `choose_path` and `num_slots` descriptions |\n| `translations/en.json` | Mirror `strings.json` changes |\n| `ARCHITECTURE.md` | New file |\n| `coordinator.py` | Expand module docstring |\n| `binary_sensor.py` | Expand module docstring |\n| `providers/zwave_js.py` | Expand module docstring |\n| `providers/_base.py` | Expand module docstring |\n| `TODO.md` | Add future enhancement items |\n"
}
```

     1→"""Exceptions for lock_code_manager."""

     3→from __future__ import annotations

     5→from typing import TYPE_CHECKING

     7→from homeassistant.exceptions import HomeAssistantError
     8→
     9→if TYPE_CHECKING:
    10→    from .providers import BaseLock
    11→
    12→
    13→class LockCodeManagerError(HomeAssistantError):
    14→    """Base class for lock_code_manager exceptions."""
    15→
    16→
    17→class EntityNotFoundError(LockCodeManagerError):
    18→    """Raise when en entity is not found."""
    19→
    20→    def __init__(self, lock: BaseLock, slot_num: int, key: str):
    21→        """Initialize the error."""
    22→        self.lock = lock
    23→        self.key = key
    24→        self.slot_num = slot_num
    25→        super().__init__(f"Entity not found for lock {lock} slot {slot_num} key {key}")
    26→
    27→
    28→class LockDisconnected(LockCodeManagerError):
    29→    """Raised when lock can't be communicated with."""
    30→
    31→
    32→class ProviderNotImplementedError(LockCodeManagerError, NotImplementedError):
    33→    """
    34→    Raised when a provider method is not implemented.
    35→
    36→    This exception should be raised by BaseLock methods that must be overridden
    37→    by provider subclasses. It combines LockCodeManagerError (so the coordinator
    38→    can catch it uniformly) with NotImplementedError (for standard Python semantics).
    39→    """
    40→
    41→    def __init__(self, provider: BaseLock, method_name: str, guidance: str = ""):
    42→        """
    43→        Initialize the error.
    44→
    45→        Args:
    46→            provider: The provider instance (self).
    47→            method_name: Name of the method that needs to be implemented.
    48→            guidance: Optional guidance on how to implement the method.
    49→
    50→        """
    51→        message = f"{provider.__class__.__name__} does not implement {method_name}()."
    52→        if guidance:
    53→            message = f"{message} {guidance}"
    54→        super().__init__(message)

   579→
   580→    async def async_set_usercode(
   581→        self, code_slot: int, usercode: int | str, name: str | None = None
   582→    ) -> bool:
   583→        """
   584→        Set a usercode on a code slot.
   585→
   586→        Returns True if the value was changed, False if already set to this value.
   587→        """
   588→        # Check if the code is already set to this value (avoid unnecessary network call)
   589→        usercode = str(usercode)
   590→        try:
   591→            if (current := get_usercode(self.node, code_slot)).get("in_use"):
   592→                current_code = str(current.get("usercode", ""))
   593→                # Resolve masked codes and compare
   594→                if usercode == self._resolve_pin_if_masked(current_code, code_slot):
   595→                    _LOGGER.debug(
   596→                        "Lock %s slot %s already has this PIN, skipping set",
   597→                        self.lock.entity_id,
   598→                        code_slot,
   599→                    )
   600→                    return False
   601→        except Exception:
   602→            # If we can't check the cache, proceed with the set
   603→            pass
   604→
   605→        self._set_in_progress_code_slot = code_slot
   606→        service_data = {
   607→            ATTR_ENTITY_ID: self.lock.entity_id,
   608→            ATTR_CODE_SLOT: code_slot,
   609→            ATTR_USERCODE: usercode,
   610→        }
   611→        await self.async_call_service(
   612→            ZWAVE_JS_DOMAIN, SERVICE_SET_LOCK_USERCODE, service_data
   613→        )
   614→        # V1 locks don't reliably update the Z-Wave JS value cache after set.
   615→        # Poll the slot directly from the device to force-update the cache
   616→        # before the coordinator reads it, preventing sync loops.
   617→        # No try-except: if the poll fails, we must not proceed with the
   618→        # optimistic update since async_request_refresh() would overwrite it
   619→        # with stale cache data, reintroducing the sync loop. Letting the
   620→        # error propagate allows the sync mechanism to retry the operation.
   621→        if self._usercode_cc_version < 2:
   622→            await get_usercode_from_node(self.node, code_slot)
   623→        # Optimistic update: Z-Wave command succeeded (lock acknowledged), but the
   624→        # value cache updates asynchronously via push notification. Update coordinator
   625→        # immediately to prevent sync loops from reading stale cache data.
   626→        if self.coordinator:
   627→            self.coordinator.push_update({code_slot: usercode})
   628→        return True

```
     1→# Duplicate Code Detection + Architecture Documentation
     2→
     3→**Date:** 2026-03-28
     4→**Status:** Approved
     5→
     6→## Problem
     7→
     8→Some locks reject setting a PIN if the same code exists in another slot. When this
     9→happens, the Z-Wave service call succeeds but the lock silently rejects it. LCM sees
    10→the code isn't set, retries, and loops indefinitely.
    11→
    12→PR #899 added reactive handling via Z-Wave event 15
    13→(`NEW_USER_CODE_NOT_ADDED_DUE_TO_DUPLICATE_CODE`), but users still report infinite
    14→sync loops — likely because not all locks emit this event reliably.
    15→
    16→Additionally, the codebase lacks clarity on:
    17→- Which slots LCM sees vs manages
    18→- How data flows between coordinator, provider, and sync sensor
    19→- The managed vs unmanaged distinction (implicit, scattered across modules)
    20→
    21→## Design
    22→
    23→### 1. Pre-flight Duplicate Detection
    24→
    25→**Where:** `async_set_usercode()` in `zwave_js.py`, before the Z-Wave service call.
    26→
    27→**Logic:** Scan `self.coordinator.data` for any other slot with the same PIN value.
    28→Skip masked values (all `*`). Skip the target slot.
    29→
    30→**New exception:** `DuplicateCodeError` — raised by `async_set_usercode()` when a
    31→duplicate is found. Includes the conflicting slot number and whether it's managed or
    32→unmanaged.
    33→
    34→**Caller-specific handling:**
    35→
    36→- **Sync path** (`_perform_sync_operation`): Catches `DuplicateCodeError`, disables
    37→  the slot via the enabled switch, creates a persistent notification identifying the
    38→  conflicting slot.
    39→- **Direct path** (`text.set_value`, websocket): Lets the exception propagate — the
    40→  action fails with a clear error message. The user explicitly set the PIN, so they
    41→  should get immediate feedback, not have their slot silently disabled.
    42→
    43→**Masked slots:** For unmanaged slots with masked PINs, we can't know the actual
    44→code. The existing event 15 handler stays as a safety net for these cases. The two
    45→approaches are complementary.
    46→
    47→### 2. Config Flow Text Updates
    48→
    49→**`choose_path` step description:**
    50→
    51→> "Some locks mask stored PINs, making them invisible to external tools. Once LCM is
    52→> managing your locks, do not set codes using other tools or manually at the lock.
    53→> Clear all slots before setup. For permanent access codes, configure them through LCM
    54→> without a condition entity."
    55→
    56→**`num_slots` step description:**
    57→
    58→> "Configure slots for all codes you want on the lock (existing user codes for the
    59→> slot range specified will be cleared). You can add and remove slots later using the
    60→> integration services."
    61→
    62→### 3. Architecture Documentation
    63→
    64→**New file:** `ARCHITECTURE.md` in the repo root.
    65→
    66→Covers the system-level data flow:
    67→- What gets polled vs pushed vs hard-refreshed
    68→- Coordinator data model (all slots, managed + unmanaged)
    69→- Sync decision flow (binary sensor)
    70→- Managed vs unmanaged slot distinction
    71→- Push update processing
    72→- Masked PIN resolution
    73→- Rate limiting and serialization
    74→
    75→**Module docstrings:** Upgrade from one-liners to 2-3 sentences describing the
    76→module's role + pointer to `ARCHITECTURE.md`. Files: `coordinator.py`,
    77→`binary_sensor.py`, `zwave_js.py`, `_base.py`.
    78→
    79→### 4. Event 15 Handler (Keep As-Is)
    80→
    81→The reactive handler from PR #899 stays. It covers:
    82→- Locks that don't have their PINs in the coordinator (edge cases)
    83→- Race conditions (code set at keypad between read and write)
    84→- Unmanaged slots with masked PINs
    85→
    86→## Out of Scope (Future TODOs)
    87→
    88→Captured in `TODO.md`:
    89→
    90→1. **Config flow lock reset step** — Check for unmanaged codes, require reset before
    91→   continuing
    92→2. **Conflicting integration detection** — Warn if Keymaster etc. is installed
    93→3. **Update migration docs** — Remove "slowly migrate" guidance
    94→4. **"Clear all unmanaged" UI action** — Bulk cleanup of stale codes
    95→5. **Lock-specific carve-outs** — Adapt detection per lock capability
    96→
    97→## Files to Modify
    98→
    99→| File | Change |
   100→|------|--------|

Each lock gets a coordinator that holds the full slot→code mapping (managed AND unmanaged).\n\n2. **Data Flow** diagram (text):\n   ```\n   Config Entry (desired state)\n       ↓\n   Binary Sensor (sync decision)\n       ↓ set/clear\n   Provider (async_set_usercode / async_clear_usercode)\n       ↓ Z-Wave service call\n   Lock (firmware)\n       ↓ push notification / poll response\n   Coordinator (actual state: ALL slots)\n       ↓ listener notification\n   Binary Sensor (re-evaluate sync)\n   ```\n\n3. **Coordinator** — Stores `dict[int, int | str]` mapping slot number to code for ALL slots on the lock. Does not distinguish managed vs unmanaged. Push-based providers set `update_interval = None`; poll-based providers use periodic refresh.\n\n4. **Push vs Poll vs Hard Refresh**:\n   - **Push**: Z-Wave value update events → provider filters → `coordinator.push_update()`\n   - **Poll**: Coordinator's `_async_update_data()` → provider's `async_get_usercodes()` on interval\n   - **Hard refresh**: `async_hard_refresh_codes()` → refreshes Z-Wave CC values cache from device, then reads all slots\n\n5. **Managed vs Unmanaged Slots** — A slot is \"managed\" if it exists in any LCM config entry's `CONF_SLOTS` for this lock AND the corresponding LCM entities exist. Detection: `_get_slot_entity_states()` returns `None` for unmanaged. Coordinator stores both; sync only operates on managed; UI displays both.\n\n6. **Sync Decision** — Binary sensor compares desired state (PIN from text entity, active from condition) against actual state (coordinator data). If active + PIN differs → set. If inactive + code present → clear.\n\n7. **Masked PINs** — Some locks return `****` instead of actual codes. For managed slots, resolved via the text entity's expected PIN. For unmanaged slots, kept as-is (just needs non-empty to indicate \"in use\").\n\n8. **Duplicate Code Detection** — Pre-flight check in `async_set_usercode()` scans coordinator data. Event 15 handler as safety net for masked/unknown slots.\n\n9. **Rate Limiting** — All lock operations serialized via `asyncio.Lock` per provider instance, with 2-second minimum delay between operations.\n\n**Step 2: Update module docstrings**\n\nEach module gets 2-3 sentences + pointer to `ARCHITECTURE.md`:\n\n- `coordinator.py`: \"Manages the slot→code mapping for a single lock. Stores ALL slots (managed and unmanaged). See ARCHITECTURE.md for the full data flow.\"\n- `binary_sensor.py`: \"Sync sensor that compares desired state (config entries) against actual state (coordinator data) and triggers set/clear operations to reconcile. See ARCHITECTURE.md for the sync decision flow.\"\n- `providers/zwave_js.py`: \"Z-Wave JS lock provider. Handles push updates, masked PIN resolution, duplicate code detection, and rate-limited set/clear operations. See ARCHITECTURE.md for the provider's role in the data flow.\"\n- `providers/_base.py`: \"Abstract base for lock providers. Handles rate limiting, connection checking, and coordinator refresh after operations. See ARCHITECTURE.md for the provider interface contract.\"\n\n**Step 3: Run pre-commit**\n\nRun: `pre-commit run --all-files`\nExpected: PASS\n\n**Step 4: Commit**\n\n```\ndocs: add ARCHITECTURE.md and expand module docstrings\n```\n\n---\n\n### Task 7: TODO.md Already Updated\n\nThe TODOs were captured earlier in this session.     66→class DuplicateCodeError(LockCodeManagerError):
    67→    """Raised when a PIN duplicates a code in another slot on the lock."""
    68→
    69→    def __init__(
    70→        self,
    71→        code_slot: int,
    72→        conflicting_slot: int,
    73→        conflicting_slot_managed: bool,
    74→        lock_entity_id: str,
    75→    ):
    76→        """Initialize the error."""
    77→        self.code_slot = code_slot
    78→        self.conflicting_slot = conflicting_slot
    79→        self.conflicting_slot_managed = conflicting_slot_managed
    80→        self.lock_entity_id = lock_entity_id
    81→        managed_str = "managed" if conflicting_slot_managed else "unmanaged"
    82→        super().__init__(
    83→            f"Lock {lock_entity_id}: cannot set code on slot {code_slot} — "
    84→            f"PIN duplicates {managed_str} slot {conflicting_slot}"
    85→        )
    86→```

   296→
   297→```python
   298→    async def _perform_sync_operation(self, slot_state: _SlotState) -> bool:
   299→        """
   300→        Perform sync operation (set or clear usercode).
   301→
   302→        Returns True if sync was performed, False if lock disconnected.
   303→        Raises nothing on duplicate — disables slot and notifies instead.
   304→        """
   305→        try:
   306→            if slot_state.active_state == STATE_ON:
   307→                await self.lock.async_internal_set_usercode(
   308→                    int(self.slot_num), slot_state.pin_state, slot_state.name_state
   309→                )
   310→                _LOGGER.debug(
   311→                    "%s (%s): Set usercode for %s slot %s",
   312→                    self.config_entry.entry_id,
   313→                    self.config_entry.title,
   314→                    self.lock.lock.entity_id,
   315→                    self.slot_num,
   316→                )
   317→            else:
   318→                await self.lock.async_internal_clear_usercode(int(self.slot_num))
   319→                _LOGGER.debug(
   320→                    "%s (%s): Cleared usercode for %s slot %s",
   321→                    self.config_entry.entry_id,
   322→                    self.config_entry.title,
   323→                    self.lock.lock.entity_id,
   324→                    self.slot_num,
   325→                )
   326→            self._cancel_retry()
   327→            return True
   328→        except DuplicateCodeError as err:
   329→            _LOGGER.error("%s", err)
   330→            # Disable the slot — this is a permanent error, not transient
   331→            await self.hass.services.async_call(
   332→                SWITCH_DOMAIN,
   333→                SERVICE_TURN_OFF,
   334→                {ATTR_ENTITY_ID: self._enabled_entity_id},
   335→                blocking=True,
   336→            )
   337→            managed_str = "managed" if err.conflicting_slot_managed else "unmanaged"
   338→            async_create(
   339→                self.hass,
   340→                (
   341→                    f"Lock {err.lock_entity_id}: cannot set code on slot "
   342→                    f"{err.code_slot} because the same PIN is already in "
   343→                    f"{managed_str} slot {err.conflicting_slot}. "
   344→                    f"Slot {err.code_slot} has been disabled. Set a unique "
   345→                    f"code and re-enable the slot."
   346→                ),
   347→                title="Duplicate Lock Code",
   348→                notification_id=(
   349→                    f"{DOMAIN}_{err.lock_entity_id}_{err.code_slot}_dupe"
   350→                ),
   351→            )
   352→            self._cancel_retry()
   353→            return True  # Operation "handled" — don't retry
   354→        except LockDisconnected as err:
   355→            _LOGGER.debug(
   356→                "%s (%s): Unable to %s usercode for %s slot %s: %s",
   357→                self.config_entry.entry_id,
   358→                self.config_entry.title,
   359→                "set" if slot_state.active_state == STATE_ON else "clear",
   360→                self.lock.lock.entity_id,
   361→                self.slot_num,
   362→                err,
   363→            )
   364→            self._schedule_retry()
   365→            return False
   366→```
